📌 Bring two ESP32 boards — that is the only thing on this plan that needs more than one. Add a microSD card (three tasks stop at it) and two RS485 transceivers if you have them. the full packing list, part by part →

Mice — refactor plan

The working document for restructuring the module system. Written 2026-08-18. This file is the source of truth for progress, not any chat log.

Read this first — you may be a different session, or a different model

  1. The code is the source of truth, not this document. Verify before you change. Several assumptions in the original brief turned out to be false; see Verified facts.
  2. Work in code/.staging, never the main tree. Promote with python promote.py, which runs the full QC suite and copies back only if it is green.
  3. Never run promote.py --init while staging holds unpromoted work. It refreshes staging from main and will delete it. This has already destroyed a feature once.
  4. Every fix needs a QC check, and the check only counts once you have broken the fix and watched it fail. See qc/README.md.
  5. Assert on what reached the module (fake_serial.wire), never on what the UI says about itself.
  6. Talking to Gemini via agy: the prompt must contain no " character — it silently truncates there, and a truncated prompt still returns confident nonsense. Ask for FIND/REPLACE blocks, never whole files (that times out).
  7. Update the STATE block as tasks move — and put the date and time on the line you touch. Mark a task doing when you PICK IT UP, not only done when it lands, or the page cannot show that anything is happening. That block is how progress survives a context limit, and the card at the top of Progress is rendered from it.

The loop, per area

Verified facts — what the brief got wrong

Checked against the real tree. Do not rebuild things that do not exist.

The brief assumedThe code showsEvidence
connect() duplicated per module WiFi, discovery, OTA, RS485 and SD exist once, in src/core/grep, firmware/src
module management tangled with flashing Already separate in Python. They touch in 3 places, all correct coordination over one cable main.py:955-1231, :394, :1367
a "Sub-PC" tier exists No such concept. Only match in the repo is the prompt log grep, whole repo
two Python servers a 13-line stub that runpys the hub nong/main_python_set_nong/main.py:12
version redundancy Two fields, both legitimate: FW_VERSION and CAL_VERSION esp32_hardware.h:14, NongModule.h:122

The two worst real problems

  1. No authentication anywhere. WebPortal::setupRoutes (WebPortal.cpp:519-875) never calls users.verify; the module login is browser-side only (WebUI.h:775) and the card prints the default password (WebUI.h:135-137). The hub binds 0.0.0.0 with no auth. Anyone on the venue WiFi can reflash a board or drive the servos.
  2. A phone cannot discover the hub. The address exists only as a console print (main.py:1958) — on no page, no QR, no mDNS — in a product designed to be used from a phone.

Decisions already taken — do not re-open these

DecisionWhy
Restructure aggressively, area by area (architecture + UI together) User's choice. Each area is finished before the next starts.
QR code and mDNS, not one or the other Venue networks block mDNS; the QR still works there. Belt and braces.
Login before acting, enforced on the board and the hub Today there is none at all — see above.
Colour lives in ONE file, and dark stays the default Asked for 2026-08-19: a file to change only the theme or colour, so a new theme can be added later without reading anything else. shared/web/themes.css holds every colour, one block per theme; mice.css keeps shape only. Dark stays default because a bright screen spills light into the show area. The picker DISCOVERS themes from the file rather than repeating the list.
Joyful colour is decoration; state colour still means state Same conversation. A theme may bring as much colour as it likes through its own accents, but ok / warn / err keep their meaning and nothing is signalled by colour alone — otherwise a cheerful theme turns a warning into an ornament.
A remote flash logs in as the OPERATOR, not as the hub Taken 2026-08-19 while building A2-4. The confirm panel asks for a login on the PC holding the cable; it is used for that one request and never stored. The alternative, a token shared between hubs, leaves every PC holding a credential that can overwrite boards on every other PC.
Remote flash sends the image over the network So a PC that never built firmware can still flash. esptool must run on the PC holding the cable.
Remote flash confirms, naming the PC Overwriting firmware is not undoable and the board may be in someone's hands across the room.
Gemini designs the visible surfaces; Claude builds and verifies logic Evidence from 2026-08-17: Gemini's design output was better; it also proposed three CSS changes that broke the rules they aimed at, and Claude caught two bugs it introduced.
Keep both pinout images, chosen by condition The 38-pin WROOM reference is right for nong and lift (nodemcu-32s) but wrong for an ESP32-CAM. MEASURED 2026-08-20: the file is 13.4 KB, not the 133 KB written here, and it still cannot come from board flash when the page is opened directly.
Stopping is never behind a login. Starting a show, moving a joint, flashing or deleting all need one; /api/play/stop never does Claude's call during A1-3, 2026-08-18, flagged to the user rather than asked, because the safe reading is not ambiguous: a password prompt while an arm is about to hit a person or a set piece is a safety failure, not security. The worst an unauthenticated caller can do is stop the show — the same thing the power switch does, and always recoverable. Written into hub_auth.NEVER_GATED and asserted by check_hub_auth so it cannot be tidied away.
Skip a task marked needs hardware and take the next one a PC can finish; do the hardware ones when the user says the board is on the bench User's instruction, 2026-08-18. qc/lib/fake_wifi.py is a Python re-implementation of the board's HTTP routes, so a change to WebPortal.cpp can be read but not executed — calling that verified would be a lie, and waiting for hardware stops all other progress. Order inside the plan is unchanged; only what is worked FIRST changes.
Do not merge SPEED/TIME/STOP/HOME across nong and lift Same user-facing verb, unrelated code (deg/s vs PWM-and-mm/s; interpolation freeze vs motor stop). Merging makes a hardware-conditional god-function.

What needs hardware, and how much of it

Asked for 2026-08-19: which one need more than 1 hardware. Everything else in this plan a PC can finish on its own. Only two rows below need a SECOND board; the rest need one board plus a particular part.

What is actually on the bench, 2026-08-19

The user's own summary, plus what each board answered when asked. Five boards, four ways of reaching them - which is the first time this project has had more than one route to test at once.

  1. ESP32 on USB to this PC — COM9, id 1, lift, type lift, fw 1.0.0.
  2. ESP32 on RS485 through a MAX485 — COM23, id 67, nong, group mice-show, fw 1.0.0. The wiring is correct: #* PING answered @67 PONG 67 nong nong, addressed frames work, an id that is not there is silent, and a long JSON reply arrives whole. Its two servos WAIST and R_EL_R (with underscores) move on command: JOINT WAIST 100 answered OK WAIST=100.0 T=83ms and the user saw and heard it turn.
  3. ESP32-CAM on COM18, id 89, type cam. Flashed with the current build it says [cam] ready, qvga, PSRAM NO and SNAP captured 14671 bytes. The Detected camera not supported error was stale firmware, not the sensor and not the ribbon.
  4. ESP32 on external power — no cable to this PC.
  5. ESP32 on the pair PC — id 42, lab-pair, type nong, group mice-show, chip 6825DD163688, flashed with the current firmware. That PC runs no hub, only a browser, so this hub cannot see that board — see A13-4 for why a browser can never share a USB port.

Still missing: an SD card. It blocks A7-4, the photo half of A7-7 and the saved-show half of A7-5.

The packing list — what to bring, and what it unlocks

Asked for 2026-08-19: which one need SD card, which one need RS485, which one need the thing. Same facts as the table below, sorted by the PART instead of by the task, because that is the order things get packed in. Nothing here is a guess about what exists — every row names the task that cannot be finished without it.

PartHow manyWithout it, these cannot be done
ESP32 boards 2 (3 is better — one to keep as a known-good) A3-5 hotspot peers (two boards, no wiring at all) · A3-8 as the second device if a phone cannot be used. Everything else needs one board, and there are already 3 TEST boards on the bench. A24-5 drive a module over WiFi needs a logged-in nong · A24-7 the bench check that every nong answers PIN? with all 8 servo pins set. A26-30 / A26-32 nong #67 on WiFi and RS485 through the hub and Studio · A26-31 read where the arm is before a move · A26-33 / A26-34 Studio over the RS485 adapter · A26-35 Studio freeze · A26-36 Studio pose vs real arm · A26-38 robot home vs show neutral · A26-41 loop back to the start in sync · A26-42 monitor over RS485 · A26-43 zero lock login · A26-47 robot home pose on the module page · A26-45 shrug preview · A26-46 stop the robot when Studio freezes • A26-75 accounts verification on nong #67 midway bench rig.
RS485 transceivers (MAX485) — HERE, and working done A3-5 the bus half — carrying the group to modules behind a board. Nothing on a PC carries a real bus, so this is the one thing the whole QC suite cannot fake. The hotspot half of A3-5 does NOT need these.
microSD card (FAT32, in the board's slot) 1, ideally 2 A7-8 the bench proof of strip + speaker needs the card for sound, like A7-4 AudioPlayer reads its files off the card · A7-5 a saved show is loaded from it · A7-7 SNAP writes a photo to it. Also the only way to check the board page's new could not read /moves — is the SD card in? message against a real missing card rather than a forced failure.
Servos — TD8135MG relax check still open nong rig with TD8135MG, servo power supply, and access to its connectors A7-5 — the risk was a move that plays silently WRONG, and only a moving arm shows that. Settled on the bench 2026-08-21 with dummy servos on board 67: a four-step file played end to end with each step waiting for the last, and - home: T 2500 took 2093 ms where the old player threw the value away and took 80. A26-92 checks why RELAX does not release the TD8135MG and why other servos sometimes shift position when RELAX is pressed.
RGB strip (WS2812 / NeoPixel) 1 A7-4 — that moving RgbStrip into modules/lift/ still lights · A7-8 wired and proven on a real board (blocked until then).
Speaker (with the DFPlayer or amp the lift uses) 1 A7-4 — the AudioPlayer half of the same move · A7-8 bench proof · A7-9 the nong gets the same speaker support (blocked on hardware).
ESP32-CAM + its CH340 shield done — no longer needed to pack A7-7 — whether SNAP can work on a cam build at all, or whether four documents describe something that cannot exist.
A phone or second laptop 1 A3-8 the hotspot fault, as a real client · A13-8 a module on WiFi, judged from a phone · A10-3 and A9-* to look at the themes and the small screens on a real phone rather than a resized browser.
A second PC on the same WiFi 1 A13-4 commanding a module that belongs to the other PC · A15-6 taking the app off one hub and watching the other find it (the code path is checked on one PC; only the end-to-end run needs two). Two hubs on one network is the venue case, and one machine cannot fake it.
A ruler or calipers 1 A16-1 measuring the nong linkage. The numbers are the part that cannot be recovered once the board goes back. • A26-71 shrug angle measurement.
A TV or large screen (optional) 1, plus a remote or a keyboard A9-2 judging three-metre legibility · A9-3 proving the arrow-key run. Optional: both can wait, and neither blocks anything else.
Two viewers for the camera — done, no longer needed 2 A13-11 the live view refused a second viewer with 503. Settled 2026-08-21: the board still allows one reader, because it has one frame buffer, but the hub holds that one connection and feeds everybody from it. Proved against a fake camera that refuses a second watcher exactly as the board does — two viewers, one connection to the board.
A second nong board + servos on both 2 boards, 1 RS485 pair between them A20-3 the 2-ESP humanoid, for when ten joints do not fit on one board's pins. The firmware already carries it (leader forwards the resolved pose and its time; a joint whose pin is -1 belongs to the other board) and it has never once run on two real boards. What cannot be faked: whether the two stay in step over a long sequence.
PCM5102A DAC + TPA3118 30W amp + the 12V speaker 1 of each, on the LIFT board, with a 12V supply for the amp A24-41 the chain the user actually has: the ESP32 speaks I2S to the PCM5102A, the DAC's line out feeds the TPA3118, and that drives the 12V speaker. Both parts are already entries in config/amps.json, but neither describes the chain, so the wiring line a person reads names only half of it.
I2S amplifier + small speaker (MAX98357A or similar) 1 of each, and the SD card above A20-4 done 2026-09-07: wired to a real nong (id 67) and heard — the amp is here, and working. Still needed for A24-25, which reflashes that board so it carries the amp table, and for re-running the tone and the show test afterwards.
USB data cables one per board Everything. A charge-only cable is the most common cause of the board did not answer, and it looks identical.

If you bring only one thing: a second board. It unlocks the half of A3-5 that needs no wiring, and it gives A3-8 a real client. If you bring two: add the SD card — three separate tasks stop at it.

TaskBoardsAlso neededWhat it would prove
A3-5 group over RS485 and hotspot peers 2 boards Two RS485 transceiver boards (MAX485 or similar) and a two-wire A/B link between them, for the bus half. The hotspot half needs no wiring at all — two boards and no venue WiFi is enough. That a module carries its group to the modules behind it, and that peers found over a hotspot stop showing as not linked. Nothing on a PC carries a real bus, so this cannot be faked.
A3-8 the PC's internet drops while sharing its hotspot 1 board + a second device The real laptop that shows the fault, and a phone or another laptop as the client. A board can stand in for the client if a phone cannot. Whether this is a fourth cause or one of the three fixed on 2026-08-06 coming back. ping -S 192.168.137.1 is NOT a valid test — it answers from the PC itself.
A7-4 move RgbStrip and AudioPlayer into modules/lift/ 1 board An RGB strip and a speaker on it That moving the code changed nothing — a strip that lights and a sound that plays. A fake cannot show either.
A7-5 SequencePlayer stops hard-coding lift and nong verbs 1 board At least two servos wired, ideally a real nong That a saved show still runs after the verbs stop being written into the player. The risk is a move that plays silently wrong, which only a moving arm shows.
A7-7 SNAP on a cam build 1 board The ESP32-CAM (COM17 on its CH340 shield) Whether SNAP can work at all on a cam build, or whether four documents are describing something that cannot exist.
A4-3 the way back to the hub (built, PC-verified) 1 board The board on WiFi, reached directly rather than through the hub The half that runs on the chip: that the board remembers the hub which spoke to it and forgets it after five minutes. The page behaviour is already driven on a PC; this is the part a PC cannot execute.

Short answer: bring two boards and, if you can, a pair of RS485 transceivers with two wires between them. Two boards alone already unlock the hotspot-peer half of A3-5 and the A3-8 reproduction. The camera, the strip and the speaker each need only one board, but they need that part attached.

TaskStatusWhenWhat
The STATE block — the only place progress is recorded. Edit it by hand, a person or any AI; everything above re-renders from it.

Format: id: status [YYYY-MM-DD [HH:MM]] — note, one per line, continuation lines indented. A line starting # is a group heading. Status is one of todo doing qc done blocked. Use qc for work that is built and waiting on the suite or a promote — it is finished but not landed, and without it a task in flight looks like nothing happening. The timestamp is when the status last changed — when a task was picked up, or when it landed.

A0-7: done 2026-08-18 17:18  — RS485Bus::send. MY PREMISE WAS WRONG, and
                the code said so: I parked this as an RS485 copy of the A0-6
                logging bug, but Serial2 is written in exactly ONE function,
                send() serialises on sendMtx_ (created because the async web
                task and loop() both send), and the driver enable is held across
                the whole frame. Nothing can get between those two writes. The
                hole that WAS open is narrower: the lock is applied as
                `if (sendMtx_)`, so a board that never created it would skip
                locking in silence and let two tasks interleave halves of two
                frames onto a bus every module obeys. That is now reported once
                instead of ignored. check_rs485_frame.py pins the three things
                that make a frame whole — one writer, the lock held ACROSS the
                writes, the driver raised and lowered once — broken three ways,
                failed three ways. Builds; not tested on hardware

A0-12: done   2026-08-18 23:15  touches=qc/,promote.py  — a change costs two full QC runs: one by hand before promoting and
                one inside promote.py as the gate. The gate must stay FULL — three
                times on 2026-08-18 a change broke a check that looked unrelated
                (a stylesheet broke Studio's panel, the logging helper broke
                check_wifi_live, the login gate broke check_modsite_tabs) — but
                the SECOND run is pure waste. Make promote reuse a run that just
                passed on an unchanged tree, or run the fast checks first and
                stop at the first failure so a broken change fails in seconds
                instead of nine minutes. ~20 of the 50 checks launch a real
                browser and that is nearly all of the time.
                TWO SUITES CANNOT RUN AT ONCE TODAY, which is why waiting hurts:
                browser.kill() kills every Edge carrying the QC tag, so a second
                run murders the first one's browser, and every run reports
                through the same fake serial port into one shared qc_marks list.
                Fixing that — a tag per run, and marks owned by the run that
                produced them — is what would let two changes be verified in
                parallel instead of queueing
A0-13: done   2026-08-19 02:49  touches=qc/,promote.py  — MORE THAN ONE .staging, so two changes can be verified at the PROVEN, not asserted: two browser-driven suites ran at the same time in two trees, both green - 45/0 in 47.6s and 45/0 in 48.3s, 49s wall clock for what was 96s serial. Each run takes its identity from its own pid: browser.TAG carries it (kill matches on the tag, so a shared one meant one run killed the other's browser mid-measurement - which looks exactly like a flaky check), and _hub_port starts at 8700+(pid%40)*25 (two runs on 8700 could have one tree's hub answer the other tree's request, which does not fail loudly, it silently tests the wrong tree). promote.py takes --staging DIR. Promoting stays SERIAL on purpose: both trees copy into the same real tree and two promotes touching one file is a lost edit - the failure that ate the settings_shared.json fix earlier.
                same time instead of queueing behind one 10 minute suite. The
                user's idea, 2026-08-18, and two staging trees are two PROCESSES
                so they do not share qc_marks or the fake port — the collision
                that bit us inside ONE process does not apply. Four things stop
                it today, all small and all verified:
                  * qc/lib/browser.py TAG is the constant MICEQCBROWSER and
                    kill() matches on it, so one run kills the other run's
                    browser — needs a per-process suffix;
                  * qc/lib/qc.py _hub_port starts at 8700 in both, so the two
                    trees fight over ports and one hub can answer the other's
                    requests — base it on the pid or take a free port;
                  * the QC auth file is named mice_qc_auth_.json, so the
                    same port means the same file (falls out of the port fix);
                  * promote.py hardcodes STAGING = MAIN/.staging — needs to take
                    the tree as an argument.
                Then promote them one at a time, since both copy into the same
                real tree and two promotes touching one file is a lost edit.
                HOW MANY. Measured on this machine 2026-08-18: 24 cores, 31 GB
                RAM, 47.8 GB free, and a staging tree is 648 MB of which 624 MB
                is firmware/.pio — the SOURCE is about 24 MB. So disk allows
                ~70 trees and the CPU is not the constraint either. The real
                ceilings are different:
                  * LOAD MAKES BROWSER CHECKS FLAKE. Every intermittent failure
                    chased on 2026-08-18 was load-induced timing. Twenty suites
                    at once is twenty times that load on the checks that were
                    just stabilised, so the number must rise only as the flake
                    causes are proven fixed;
                  * INDEPENDENT WORK RUNS OUT. Parallel trees only help when the
                    tasks touch different files, and this plan does not have
                    twenty such streams — every A2 task edits hub.html and every
                    A4 task edits WebUI.h. Three to five is what actually exists;
                  * and one build cache would save most of the disk anyway:
                    point PLATFORMIO_CORE_DIR at a shared folder so the trees do
                    not each carry 624 MB of the same libraries.
                So: start at THREE, measure wall-clock and how many runs come
                back green first time, and raise it only if that stays clean.
                Twenty trees on a suite that flakes under load is twenty times
                the debugging, not twenty times the work

# A1  Authentication — there is none today
A1-1: done   2026-08-19 00:57  touches=WebPortal.cpp  hw=1 board (done on nong id 85, 2026-08-19) — [hw] runs in WebPortal.cpp on the board; fake_wifi is a Python stand-in, so auth there can be read but never executed. Original task: enforce auth on the board's mutating HTTP routes VERIFIED ON REAL HARDWARE 2026-08-19, nong id 85 lift-test at 192.168.137.72. Before: GET /api/cmd?c=SET NAME hacked returned 200 and ran, from any device on the WiFi. After: 401. Reading stays open (PING, INFO, /api/status, the page, the stylesheet) because the hub's own discovery probes /api/status and gating it would hide every board. STOP stays open on purpose - refusing a stop is the dangerous answer. What is gated comes from config/commands.json (query / safety flags compiled into COMMAND_DOCS), not a list in C++, so allowing a new command is one entry and an unknown command fails CLOSED.                 hub_auth.py holds the gate; nothing that CHANGES anything is

                still is. Three things the obvious design would have got wrong:
                (1) the rule is not POST — /api/usb/cmd, /api/robot/cmd and
                /api/robot/delete are GETs that move servos and delete files, so
                the gate is a LIST of what changes something; (2) STOPPING is
                never gated (NEVER_GATED), because a password prompt while an arm
                is about to hit someone is a safety failure — starting a show
                needs a login, ending one never does; (3) a module on another PC
                is gated by THAT hub, since a session here means nothing there,
                and the error says to go and log in on that PC. Password is
                PBKDF2 + random salt, generated on first run and printed only in

                no one else. check_hub_auth.py drives it from outside with no

SNAP  on the cam board can now be tested, which was the only part of A7-7 still waiting for hardware SNAP REGRESSED ON THE BENCH, and it looks like power rather than code. Earlier today, same firmware, SNAP answered OK captured 14671 bytes. Now it answers ERR capture failed every time, including straight after a reboot, while the camera itself still initialises - the boot line says [cam] ready, qvga, PSRAM NO. What changed is the bench, not the build: there are now four boards and a servo rig on this PC's USB. An ESP32-CAM draws about 300 mA in bursts while clocking the sensor and compressing, far more than at init, so a rail that is fine for booting can fail a capture. TO TEST: give the CAM its own port or a powered hub, then SNAP again. SNAP  correctly answers ERR no sd card - that board has no card, and the honest error is the right one DONE ON HARDWARE, and the user's hunch was right: it is a different board. The camera reports pid 0x5640 - an OV5640, not the OV2640 everything assumed. Two faults followed from that. CAM never reported the sensor at all, so a board that initialised and then failed every capture gave no clue which part it was; it now says board, sensor, pid and version. And the start frame size was ONE hardcoded value, SVGA, measured on an OV2640, applied to every board while the frame buffer stayed allocated for the init size - on a five megapixel part with no PSRAM that is exactly how a capture fails after a clean init. The size is now a table keyed on the sensor, and an unmeasured part keeps the size its buffer was allocated for. Result: 6 captures out of 6, ~3.2 KB each, where before it failed every time. Also asked for: work with boards nobody has identified yet. The pin MAP is now a table too - ai-thinker, esp-eye, m5stack, m5stack-wide, ttgo-journal - tried in order until the camera answers. Proved by deliberately breaking the compiled-in pins: the board came back saying board=ai-thinker and captured 2986 bytes

A13-2: done   2026-08-19 15:02  touches=firmware,main.py hw=RS485 + nong id 67 (here)  — RS485 is real now, so the things a FOUND A REAL BUG THE FAKES COULD NEVER SHOW. On the bench bus the nong (id 67) answers a broadcast after 1344 ms, because RS485Bus.cpp:62 staggers replies by id*20 ms to avoid collisions - and main.py:1618 listens for 0.8 s. So the hub discovers only ids up to 40, while default ids run 1-247: most boards are invisible on a real bus. The hub sees the two USB boards and not the nong, with no error, which reads as an empty bus. Measured, not theorised: rts and dtr make no difference, the reply is 1344 ms every time, and 67 x 20 = 1340 MEASURED, and one half is still unmeasured, said plainly. The OLD stagger is confirmed on real hardware: the nong on id 67 answers a bus broadcast at 1344 ms, which is 67 x 20. The NEW bounded stagger cannot be measured on the bus yet, because the only board on the bus is the nong and it cannot be reflashed - it is on RS485, not USB, and esptool needs a direct UART. A board on USB answers a broadcast in 3-6 ms whatever its id, because the USB path does not stagger at all; that is the transport, not the fix. So: hub side measured and fixed, firmware side written and checked against a fake that now staggers, and honestly not yet proved on a real bus NOW MEASURED ON THE REAL BUS, both halves. The nong (id 67) was reflashed over USB and put back on RS485. Broadcast reply BEFORE: 1344 ms, which is 67 x 20. AFTER: 188, 195, 193 and 196 ms across four runs, against a design figure of (67 % 24) x 10 = 190. So the bounded stagger is real, not just modelled - every board now answers a broadcast inside the hub's floor whatever its id. The same board also reports chip B4BFE91C5A74 over the bus, so A3-4 is verified through RS485 as well as over USB. Why the flash had to happen over USB is worth keeping: esptool needs the ROM bootloader, entered by holding GPIO0 low during reset via DTR and RTS, and RS485 has no such lines to EN or IO0
                fake bus could never show can be measured: an addressed command
                to #67, a broadcast that several boards answer, and what happens
                when two answers collide. The hub already speaks this protocol;
                what has never been proved is that it survives a real bus

PRIORITY RAISED 2026-08-20 by the user: *make it can flash through all this 3 method too if i need to shieft to stm 32 it cannot use wifi it can use only rs485*. That settles the design question this task had left open. esptool is not the answer and never can be over RS485 - the ROM bootloader needs DTR and RTS on EN and IO0, and the bus has no such lines - so the update has to live INSIDE the firmware: the hub sends the image as ordinary commands (FBEGIN / FDATA / FEND), the board writes it into its spare OTA slot, verifies it, and reboots into it. The point of doing it that way is that the protocol is transport-agnostic: the same commands ride a USB cable, the RS485 bus, or WiFi, and a part that cannot do WiFi at all is then still updatable. Testable TODAY over USB, because the command channel is the same one - so the bus is only the last step, not the first. PROVEN ON REAL HARDWARE 2026-08-20. Firmware now travels as ordinary commands (FWBEGIN/FWDATA/FWEND), so the transport stops mattering. Board 85 over a USB cable: app0 -> app1, 1.29 MB in 239 s. Board 1 over RS485 ONLY, through a CH340+MAX485 dongle as usb:COM23:1 - no USB serial to that board and no radio anywhere in the path: app0 -> app1, 1.43 MB in 282 s. Three real bugs the bench found and a fake never would: (1) a chunk truncated by 64 characters is still VALID base64, so it decoded to 102 bytes instead of 150 and nothing noticed until FWEND counted the image 48 bytes short - each chunk now declares its length and a short one is refused without advancing, so the sender simply repeats it; (2) the hub required the firmware's blank-line marker before any reply, which a plain dongle cannot produce because there is no board on the cable to add it - it read @1 PONG 1 lift lift and reported 'no reply from COM23', so the @ prefix is now its own marker; (3) FWEND would have timed out at the 2 s default while the board hashed 1.3 MB, reporting a FAILED update for one that succeeded. Also landed with it: a brownout guard (firmware/src/core/BrownoutGuard.h) so a board whose supply cannot start it comes up with the radio OFF and stays answerable on USB and RS485 instead of resetting forever; the hub now counts boots and names the power rail instead of saying 'no reply'; a USB-RS485 DONGLE's replies are no longer discarded for lacking the firmware's blank-line marker (the @ prefix is its own marker); the flash watcher finished only on FAILURE because it waited for a 'done' key /api/flash has never sent, so a successful write left the button disabled and never applied the board's type; and ai_panel.py stopped reporting outages as reviews (tool errors were parsed as findings, the head printed '(nothing)' when it never ran, and --dir listed only the top level so a question about BusUpdate.cpp reported CHANGELOG.md as its scope). ALL THREE FLASH METHODS RE-PROVEN 2026-08-21 on today's build, and every board on the bench updated. USB/esptool: lift 1 (COM9, mice_lift), nong 85 lift-test (COM26), nong 42 lab-pair (COM28), cam 89 (COM18, mice_cam), nong 67 (COM29), and the spare (COM27). All six now answer FWSTAT idle, so the one-time esptool bootstrap is done for every board here - 42 and 89 had been running firmware with no updater at all (ERR unknown cmd: FWSTAT). THE BOARD ON COM27 IS ALIVE AGAIN: the plan had it as a 30-pin ESP32 that boot-loops on brownout, and it did - Brownout detector was triggered, empty NVS, resetting forever.                 it. Asked 2026-08-19: why can we not upload over RS485 or WiFi.
                WiFi already works - the board has OTA and writes the spare app
                slot, so a failed update keeps the firmware it has. RS485 cannot
                use esptool at all: the ROM bootloader is entered by holding
                GPIO0 low during reset through DTR and RTS, and a differential
                pair has no such lines. But the running firmware already accepts
                a file over the bus (FBEGIN FDATA FEND) and already knows how to
                write an OTA slot, so the two joined would be a real bus update
                with no ROM bootloader involved. The board must be able to
                refuse a bad image and keep running the old one, exactly as OTA
                does now

A13-10: done   2026-08-19 18:50  touches=main.py  — plain mice.local, with no port. Asked 2026-08-19: DONE 2026-08-19 and proved both ways on this machine. http://mice.local now works with no port: a second listener takes port 80 and only redirects, so the hub stays on 8642. Measured - 302 to mice.local:8642 and the hub page lands. The half that matters more was tested by taking port 80 away with a socket first: the hub starts exactly as before and the short name simply does not exist. Moving the hub TO port 80 would have meant a PC with IIS or Skype could not run it at all. One real Windows trap found on the way: ThreadingHTTPServer sets SO_REUSEADDR, and on Windows that is not the harmless TIME_WAIT reuse it is on Unix - it lets one process bind a port another process is LISTENING on, and then connections go to whichever. The redirect listener sets allow_reuse_address False so it can never steal port 80 from another server. Three sabotages, all caught TWO LIMITS, both deliberate. Port 80 is per machine: if something holds it on the other PC, that PC keeps the port in its address while this one does not - the hub must never fail to start over a convenience. And mice.local is one name: two hubs on the SAME network both answer it and whichever replies first wins, at random. That cannot happen on this bench because the two PCs are on different subnets and mDNS is link-local, but it would on one network. The answer is not a name to remember - mice-msi.local was rejected for exactly that - it is that mice.local means the hub on the machine you are at, and other PCs are LINKS in the Other PCs list, named by their own hostname and clicked rather than typed
                why type mice.local:8642. Because a bare name means port 80 and
                the hub is on 8642. Do NOT move the hub to 80 - on Windows
                something else often holds it (IIS, Skype, another dev server)
                and the hub would fail to start at all, which reads as the app
                being broken. Instead take port 80 as a SECOND listener that
                only redirects to the real one, and if the port is already
                taken, carry on silently: the hub still works, the short name
                just does not. It must never delay startup waiting for a port
                it may not get

A13-12: done   2026-08-19 20:37  touches=mdns.py,main.py hw=two hubs on one link (here)  — two hubs, one name. Raised Asked whether hubs should be picked by speed, like the internet choosing the fastest server. They should not, and the reason is worth keeping: hubs are NOT copies of each other. Each one owns its own cables, so a board plugged into the MSI can only be DRIVEN by the MSI - that is physics, not policy. What they share is visibility: any hub lists every board across every linked hub, and forwards a command to the machine holding the cable (dev = hub:IP/usb:COMx). So open whichever hub is nearest, it is a front door for all of them; the only differences are one network hop for a far board, and that a board is reachable only while its own PC is running a hub. That is exactly why the fix is NAMING rather than load balancing - you need to know which machine will do the work, not to be sent to the quickest one

(2) OLLAMA is installed on this PC with qwen2.5-coder:7b and qwen2.5-coder:3b-base - free per token and unaffected by the network, which matters because an unreliable link is not free: the panel run at 18:36 spent 603,425 tokens and returned nothing, since every model read files for 400s and only THEN hit the auth error. The catch with ollama is that it cannot read files itself, so a local voice has to be given the text - fine for two or three files, not for a whole tree. MAKER DIVERSITY IS WEAKER EVIDENCE THAN I CLAIMED - the user caught this 2026-08-20. check_local_panel asserted that no model family may appear twice, on the theory that two models from one maker share their blind spots and the second teaches nothing. This project's own evidence contradicts it: on 2026-08-20 a SONNET subagent and an OPUS subagent - same maker - found completely non-overlapping bugs (sonnet: md5 was optional so a corrupted image could boot; opus: NUL boot lines returned as replies, and forwarded WiFi commands never got PEER_WAIT). The honest caveat is that those two were also given DIFFERENT FILES to read, so it was never a clean test of maker-vs-maker. What it does suggest is that SCOPE diversity is the stronger lever: two models on the same 200 lines converge, two models on different files cannot. Relax the check to a floor of distinct makers, drop the no-duplicates claim, and consider giving each panel model its own slice of the tree instead of the same prompt. MEASURED 2026-08-20 22:30 - WHAT LOCAL MODELS ARE AND ARE NOT FOR. Benchmark: the pre-fix BusUpdate.cpp with the md5 hole a Sonnet subagent had found hours earlier, same file, question 'can this board end up running a corrupted image, name the line'. deepseek-coder-v2:16b and qwen2.5-coder:14b, each under the terse ai_brief and under a reasoning-allowed brief. Four runs, FOUR MISSES - and not quiet misses: deepseek quoted the written_ != total_ guard and called it the line that ALLOWS corruption (it prevents it), then claimed lines 59/65/73 lacked base64 and length checks that are written there; qwen said a sender would have to fail the final md5 check, ASSUMING the md5 was registered, which is exactly the bug. Both pattern-matched Update.end(true) to md5-verified without tracing whether setMD5 was ever called. Caveat on fairness: they were given only BusUpdate.cpp while Sonnet could also read CommandRouter.cpp and see the md5 arg was optional - but the md5_.length() == 32 line was in the file they had, so it was findable. CONCLUSION: do not use local models to answer is-this-code-correct. Use them for lookups whose answer can be checked in seconds, and for throwing up alternatives where a wrong idea is cheap. Every finding checked against the code, which is already the rule and now has a number behind it. LOCAL MODELS: MEASURED, THEN DELETED, 2026-08-20. 100 cases with answers computed from the real tree before asking - five job kinds, twenty each, nothing graded by another model. Scores (fact = read a constant's value / exists / classify an error / count occurrences / extract the failing check from a log): phi4:14b 100/55/80/15/25, qwen2.5-coder:14b 100/85/35/25/15, llama3.1:8b 100/55/75/5/15, deepseek-coder-v2:16b 100/60/60/20/5, qwen2.5-coder:7b 60/50/20/0/45. Exactly ONE job clears 90% and four models are perfect at it. THE POINT THAT SETTLED IT: even that job is one grep already does better. To ask a model for a constant's value, something must first FIND the line and hand it the snippet - and the grep that finds it has already printed the answer. The model adds a load, a step, and a chance to be wrong to a question answered for free. Combined with the earlier result (0 of 8 valid runs on a real bug, with confident false findings about guards written in front of them), the conclusion is that local models save nothing on this project: not review, not search, not lookups, not comments - the comments here carry measurements and dates that live in the session's context, not in the code. The four review models were deleted, freeing 32 GB; qwen2.5-coder:7b and 3b-base stay because the USER uses them directly. tools/local_panel.py and check_local_panel were removed with them. Do not rebuild this without new evidence. EXPLAINED and guarded 2026-08-21. Three browser checks failed intermittently in parallel gates on 2026-08-20 (check_flash_confirm, check_responsive, check_flash_remote) and every one passed alone. Two causes, both found: (1) MY OWN LOAD - the gate that failed check_flash_remote with ConnectionAbortedError was running while ollama held the GPU at 96% and a 9 GB model download had the network; the same check passed alone in 11.9 s straight afterwards. That is now a rule in CLAUDE.md: nothing heavy runs during a gate, and .staging is never edited during one. It caught the regression when the old window was put back.

A13-15: done   2026-08-20 23:57  — RS485 replies arrive corrupted, and the fix is written but cannot be proved from this desk. MEASURED 2026-08-20 at the bench: every reply over RS485 came back with runs of NUL bytes replacing characters - even a 20-byte PONG read PO then four NULs then ined cam - while two boards on plain USB cables answered byte-identically through the same hub process. The five-model panel found the cause in RS485Bus::send: flush() empties the FIFO but not the shift register, and the code then waited 20us before dropping the driver enable, when one byte at 115200 takes 86.8us. Half a character on the wire is a framing error, and a framing error arrives as 0x00. The hold is now two byte periods derived from RS485_BAUD (172us), held by check_rs485_turnaround. TO VERIFY, one wiring change is needed: the two boards on the bus (#67 and #89) can only be reached over RS485, and firmware cannot be flashed that way (A13-6). Either put the A/B pair on board #85, which is on COM25 and already runs this firmware, or give #67 a USB cable for one flash. Then read INFO over the bus and count NULs - it was 100 percent corrupted before NOT done - the fix is written, checked and landed, but NOTHING has proved it on a real bus. Blocked 2026-08-20 at the user's word: they have no spare MAX485 for the two ESP32s yet and will say when they do. Until then the corruption measured today stands unexplained-in-practice: the theory is strong (20us hold against an 86.8us byte) and the evidence fits (NUL runs, only on RS485, never on a direct cable), but a theory that fits is not a measurement. UNBLOCKED 2026-08-20: the user has a spare 30-pin ESP32 and another MAX485. Wiring given: GPIO16=RO, GPIO17=DI, GPIO4=DE and /RE tied, GND common to every transceiver, 5V to VCC, A and B onto the existing pair, and leave the board on USB so it can be flashed. That makes the test clean rather than merely possible: the new board runs the FIXED turnaround while #67 and #89 still run the old one, on the same wires at the same time - so the comparison is same-bus, same-moment, and no wiring change can be blamed for the difference. Measure: read INFO over the bus 5 times from each and count NUL bytes. Before, every RS485 reply was corrupted; a direct cable was byte-identical twice. 2026-08-20 16:5x: the 30-pin board is wired and FLASHED with the fixed firmware (mice_nong, upload verified), but it cannot run - the serial output is 'Brownout detector was triggered' repeating, so it resets before it can answer. That is power, not firmware. Most likely the MAX485 is on the 3.3V pin rather than VIN/5V; those modules are 5V parts and pull more than the devkit regulator gives. A18-1: done   2026-08-21 05:33  — SECURITY: /api/ota writes firmware BEFORE the login is checked. allowed(req) sits in the completion handler, but AsyncWebServer runs the BODY handler first - so Update.end(true) has already switched the boot partition when the 401 is sent. A18-2: done   2026-08-21 16:58  — one wedged board must not hang the hub. FOUND ON HARDWARE 2026-08-21: a board on COM26 sent 23,395 lines in three seconds and never paused, and a probe of six ports never returned - five minutes, no answer, no error. Cause is _usb_get in main.py: the settle wait pushed its own deadline forward on every arriving byte, so a cable that never goes quiet held it forever, and because the scan walks every port in turn ONE noisy board froze the whole module list. At a venue that is a hub that looks dead with every other board working. Fixed with an absolute 4s cap on top of the 1.2s settle window. check_chatty_board holds it, driven by a fake cable that never stops talking (fake_serial.FLOOD_PORT, which no fake had ever done - 3120 passing checks said nothing about this). A18-3: done   2026-08-21 17:33  — the hub could not update a board over WiFi at all - found on real hardware 2026-08-21. A18-1 moved the board's login check into the OTA body handler, which is right: it refuses BEFORE any byte is written. But the hub never logs in to a board, it just posts the image, so every up-to-date board answered 401 ERR log in first. It looked like it worked before only because the old code wrote the image FIRST and refused afterwards - the update landed, the hub reported an error, and the two cancelled out. Reproduced on board 42 over WiFi, then fixed: the hub logs in to the board (POST /api/login user+pass, cookie mice_board) and carries that session on the OTA, defaulting to the shipped account and saying plainly when a board keeps its own. Verified on five real boards over WiFi: id 1 lift 21s, id 42 nong 16s, id 67 nong 16s, id 89 cam 16s, id 77 cam 14s. THE FIX HAD A BUG OF ITS OWN, also found on hardware: camera id 77 runs firmware old enough to have no /api/login, answered 404, and insisting on a session stranded it on old firmware - the very board that most needed updating. A 404 now means carry on without a session, which is what its ungated /api/ota expects. QC could not have caught either half: fake_wifi took an unauthenticated image happily, so it now demands the session the real board demands and can also play an old board with no login route. Two sabotages, both caught. STILL OPEN: board 85 lift-test has a password nobody here knows, so it cannot be updated over WiFi - USB and the RS485 command channel still work on it (FWBEGIN answers a usage error, not ERR auth)

A18-8: done   2026-08-21 22:35  — check_no_undefined_names is fragile under load and has cost three gates in one day. It drives several pages in one browser run and fails with reject:Failed~to~fetch when the machine is busy - 6/6 passing alone in 18 s, 4/6 in 62 s under a full gate. A fetch that never completed says nothing about undefined names, which is what the check is for, so it should tell a connection failure apart from a page that threw: retry the fetch once, and if it still cannot connect say the machine was too busy rather than reporting the page as broken. A red gate that is really contention sends the next half hour into code that was never wrong DONE 2026-08-21. The check now classifies before it judges: a transport failure (failed to fetch, a network error, a page that never loaded) gets ONE reload, and if it still cannot be reached the page is reported NOT PROVEN rather than broken - a pass that says out loud that nothing was tested. A throw from the page's own code is never retried and never softened. Three probe iframes make both halves happen on purpose in a real browser: busy probe fetches a dead port on every attempt and must come back busy, busy once does it only on the first attempt and must be rescued by the reload (tries=2), throw probe calls a name that does not exist and must still be reported as a throw and never retried (tries=1). Three sabotages, three caught (no retry, a fetch failure counted as a page throw, every error softened to busy). Same 17s runtime as before, because the reload only happens for pages that could not be reached.

                2026-08-19: mice.local:8642 does not fix the ambiguity, because
                the PORT was never the ambiguous part - the NAME is. Two hubs on
                one network both answer mice.local and whichever replies first
                wins, differently each lookup.
                Do what Bonjour does: PROBE before claiming. A hub asks whether
                mice.local is already answered by someone else; if it is, it
                does not answer for it and claims mice-.local instead.
                The first hub on a network then owns the short name outright,
                every other hub has a unique one, and nobody has to remember a
                unique one because other PCs are links in the Other PCs list.
                Testable here, unlike on the bench: two hubs on THIS machine
                share a link, which is exactly the case two PCs on different
                subnets cannot reproduce

A24-32: done   2026-09-07 20:48  — user 2026-09-07: how to make it run voice follow the pc like the bluetooth speaker - i.e. the robot's speaker plays what the PC is playing/saying, live, instead of only files from its own SD card. THREE WAYS, to be decided: (a) SPEAK-AND-SEND, buildable today with no new firmware: the PC renders the phrase to a wav, uploads it to /music through the hub and sends PLAY - about a second of delay, perfect sync with a show, and it reuses A24-22 and A24-26 whole; (b) STREAM over WiFi: the board takes a chunked POST of PCM into a ring buffer and feeds I2S - real live audio, but 705 kbps for 44k mono and the board already stops answering HTTP while it decodes from the card, so buffering and dropouts are the whole problem; (c) A2DP Bluetooth sink so the PC pairs with it as a real bluetooth speaker - the ESP32 can do it, but BT classic beside WiFi on a board at 76 percent flash with ten servos is the risky one. Recommend (a) first, then (b) if live is really needed. Panel first, then build DECIDED by the user 2026-09-07: stream over wifi - option (b). So the board takes live audio from the PC and plays it as it arrives, like a bluetooth speaker. Constraints already measured on the real board: it stops answering HTTP for the whole length of a track while AudioPlayer holds the SD card under the SPI mutex, flash is at 76.3 percent, and ten servo signals plus WiFi are already running on it. Design going to the panel first DESIGN from Gemini Pro 2026-09-07, four answers: (1) UDP, not TCP and not a websocket on the async server - a TCP retransmission stalls playback and the async server already starves the CPU; UDP drops a late packet instead of holding the whole stream up. (2) 22050 Hz 16-bit mono PCM, uncompressed so nothing has to decode: 352 kbps. (3) 200 ms of buffer = 8820 bytes of RAM; on underrun feed silence to I2S, stop reading, and wait until the buffer is half full again before resuming. (4) The thing most likely to kill it: WiFi latency spikes plus interrupt contention - the servo timers, RS485 and the web server starving the audio task - and the way to know early is to MEASURE the longest gap between I2S writes. So the board will report that gap and its underrun count in status, and that number is the acceptance test. BUILD ORDER: firmware core/AudioStream (UDP listener, ring buffer, stats) driving AudioPlayer's existing I2S output so pins, mono and volume are not duplicated; STREAM ON/OFF/? in commands.json; then a hub sender - a wav file first to prove the path, then the browser capturing the PC's own audio the way rgb.html already does with getDisplayMedia LANDED 2026-09-07, gate 4424/0, promoted 19 files, board flashed and hub restarted. Live audio works PC to robot: core/AudioStream (UDP 4210, 22050 Hz 16-bit mono, 200 ms ring, silence-and-refill on underrun) fed by main_python/stream_audio.py, paced one 20 ms datagram at a time. MEASURED ON THE BOARD: 250 datagrams, 0 dropped, 0 lost, longest feed gap 9 ms against a 200 ms buffer, with ten servos, RS485 and the web server all running. Two gates were red first and only the first was real - three empty catch blocks in the cast script (caught by check_modsite_errors, the check written for exactly that) and a temp folder created inside main_python that crashed the build-stamp checks in a full run while passing alone. The second red was a flake: same code passed on the next run

A24-47: done   2026-09-08 13:46  — found while landing the panel fixes 2026-09-08: THREE gates in two days died on the QC hub failing to bind its port - check_flash_remote, check_responsive (WinError 10061 connection refused) and check_themes (WinError 10013 forbidden). One cause: _free_port() asks the OS for a port and CLOSES it before main.py binds it again, and in that gap Windows can hand the same number to another worker or fold it into a reserved range (WinNAT/Hyper-V). The hub thread then dies on bind and start_hub waits six seconds before every request answers connection refused - which reads as a broken check rather than a busy machine, and costs a whole 11-minute gate to learn nothing. FIXED: start_hub watches the thread, takes a fresh port when it dies, tries four times, and says plainly when no port can be had. check_qc_parallel holds it (1 of 1 sabotage caught, after a first version that was too loose to bite)

ANSWERING THE USER 2026-09-23 (why not use the SolidWorks URDF export?): I was WRONG that sw2urdf cannot be scripted. SW2URDF 1.6 (C:/Program Files/SolidWorks Corp/SolidWorks/URDFExporter/SW2URDF.DLL) ships a public .NET API - ExportHelper (CreateRobotFromTreeView, ExportRobot, EstimateGlobalJointFromComponents, GetRefAxes), ConfigurationSerialization (LoadBaseNodeFromModel / SaveConfigTreeXML) and CSV.ImportExport (LoadURDFRobotFromCSV). Driving it needs C# compiled in-process: PowerShell cannot QueryInterface a __ComObject to ISldWorks, and the ExportHelper ctor wants the SldWorks coclass interface, not ISldWorks. Working probe: scratchpad/sw2urdf_probe.ps1. TWO FINDINGS: (1) nong_assembly.SLDASM has NO saved URDF tree - LoadBaseNodeFromModel returns null, so nobody has run the wizard on it; (2) CreateRobotFromActiveModel throws NullReferenceException when called from OUTSIDE SolidWorks even after UpdateReferenceGeometries - the add-in expects to be in-process (its own log4net appender cannot even write from another process). CONCLUSION: the wizard must be run ONCE by hand to define the joint tree; it is then saved inside the assembly, and after that LoadBaseNodeFromModel + ExportRobot re-export from a script forever, so a design change becomes one command. docs/urdf_nong.md has the exact table to type into that one wizard pass. In nong/main_python_set_nong/models/, 27.6 MB total. What the multi-model review has actually caught

Kept as evidence, because the cost of asking two or three other models is a few minutes and the argument for it is only as good as its record. Every one of these was verified against the code before it was acted on, and every one was in code written the same day, with its own QC check already green.

FoundWhat it was
main.cpp:142 unbounded line buffer Claude Opus 4.6. A USB host that never sends a newline grew a String until the heap died. The guard already existed twenty lines away in RS485Bus.cpp:25; the USB reader beside it never got one.
app.js stale peer Gemini 3.7 Flash. window.HUB_PEER was set once at page load and never cleared, so changing the cable by hand kept sending commands to the module behind the OLD board's hotspot.
app.js module link drops the peer Same review. The open-the-website link pointed at the plugged-in board while the commands went to the peer — two robots, one screen.
hub.html false empty state Same review. Home painted No modules found yet before the scans it counts had answered.

Two of those three JavaScript faults are the same shape: a value that describes ONE session, kept in a global that nothing resets. Worth remembering next time a page grows a window.SOMETHING.

Working in parallel — what can go together

One QC run is 9 to 14 minutes and the gate must stay FULL (three times on 2026-08-18 a change broke a check that looked unrelated). So the saving is not in running less: it is in putting SEVERAL finished tasks through ONE gate. A0-7 + A0-8 + A0-9 cost one gate instead of three.

That only works when the tasks do not touch the same file. These are the lanes. Take at most one task from each lane into a batch, and edits inside a lane stay strictly one at a time.

LaneTasks that live there
main_python/web/hub.html A2-1 A2-2 A2-3 A2-5 A3-6 A6-1 A6-2 A6-3
firmware C++ (src/) A3-5 A7-1 A7-2 A7-3 A7-4 A7-5 A7-6 A7-7
main_python/main.py A2-4 A3-1 A3-2 A3-3 A3-4 A8-1 A8-2
firmware/src/web/WebUI.h A4-1 A4-2 A4-3 A4-4 A4-5
shared/web/mice.cssA9-2 A9-4
qc/A0-13 A9-1 A9-5
Nong StudioA4-6 A8-3

The ones that cross lanes — these run ALONE

A0-10 (three tab systems) touches hub.html, WebUI.h and Studio at once. A0-12 touches QC and the firmware build. A9-3 and A5-1 are cross-cutting by nature. Batching one of these with anything else is how two edits become one lost edit.

And whatever is verified in parallel, promote one at a time: every tree copies into the same real tree.

Verification, every time

python qc/run_qc.py --quick        while iterating (~10 s)
pio run -e mice_nong -e mice_cam -e mice_lift -e mice_blank
python qc/run_qc.py                full, drives a real browser (~8 min)
python promote.py                  runs the suite again, copies back only if green
python -m PyInstaller --onefile --icon main_python/nong.ico \
       --name MiceHub main_python/main.py     when main.py changed

Nothing here is tested on hardware. Every check runs against fakes. A real ESP32-CAM, a nong arm and a two-PC setup are needed — especially for area 1 (auth) and area 7 (firmware restructure).
